## [2026-05-17] ingest | Dive into Systems — Ch 13.2 Processes

- Source: web URL `https://diveintosystems.org/book/C13-OS/processes.html` → fetched via WebFetch. **Second leaf of Ch 13 *The Operating System*** of *[[DiveIntoSystems]]* — **119th ingested DIS chapter**.
- New source page: `wiki/sources/dis-13-2-processes.md` — codifies the [[Process|process]] as *"an instance of a program running in the system, which includes the program's binary executable code, data, and execution context"*; the four-state [[ProcessState|process-state machine]] (Ready / Running / Blocked / Exited); the per-process [[ProcessControlBlock|PCB]] (PID + address space + register snapshot + open files + state); the **mechanism / policy split** ([[ContextSwitch|context switch]] = how, [[Scheduler|scheduling]] = which); [[Multiprogramming|multiprogramming]] (switch on I/O block) + [[Timesharing|timesharing]] (quantum-bounded preemption); the POSIX [[SystemCall|syscall]] triad [[Fork|`fork`]] / [[Exec|`exec`]] / [[Wait|`wait`]] with the six-interleaving worked print example and the foreground vs background shell story; [[Zombie|zombies]] + [[Init|`init` (PID 1)]] as the adoption safety valve; the [[WallTime|wall time]] vs [[CPUTime|CPU time]] benchmarking distinction.
- **11 new concept pages minted**:
  - [[ProcessState]] — four-state life cycle with explicit transitions.
  - [[ProcessControlBlock]] — kernel per-process struct; the destination of context-switch register saves.
  - [[Exec]] — POSIX image-replacement syscall family (`execvp` / `execve` / ...).
  - [[Wait]] — POSIX child-reaping syscall (`wait` / `waitpid`); paired with `SIGCHLD`.
  - [[Scheduler]] — policy half of the mechanism/policy split.
  - [[ProcessScheduling]] — the policy catalog; DIS 13.2 explicitly defers specifics to an OS textbook.
  - [[Multiprogramming]] — keep multiple processes resident; switch on I/O block.
  - [[Zombie]] — terminated-but-unreaped state.
  - [[Init]] — PID 1; root of the process hierarchy + orphan adopter.
  - [[WallTime]] — Running + Blocked + Ready clock; contaminated by concurrent processes.
  - [[CPUTime]] — Running-only clock; clean across concurrent processes; can exceed wall time on multicore.
- **2 concept pages extended in place**: [[Process]] (full Ch 13.2 treatment — state machine + PCB + fork/exec/wait API + mechanism/policy split, keeping the prior [[parproc-ch01-intro-parallel-processing|ParProc Ch 1]] thread-vs-process framing); [[Fork]] (Ch 13.2 semantics — exact-copy of address/execution state, six-interleaving worked example, init-as-root claim).
- Entity update: [[DiveIntoSystems]] `chapters_ingested` 118 → 119; new Ch 13.2 connection entry at top of Connections list; source slug prepended to `sources:` array.
- Validation: all 11 new pages exist on disk; index updated with source entry (count 337 → 338) and 11 concept entries; log entry added.

## [2026-05-17] ingest | Dive into Systems — Ch 11.3 Locality

- Source: web URL `https://diveintosystems.org/book/C11-MemHierarchy/locality.html` → fetched via WebFetch. **Third leaf of Ch 11 *The Memory Hierarchy*** of *[[DiveIntoSystems]]* — **108th ingested DIS chapter**.
- New source page: `wiki/sources/dis-11-3-locality.md` — codifies the **two-axis locality taxonomy** (*"the two basic forms of locality are temporal locality and spatial locality"*); locality as the *system-designer's exploit* that produces *"the illusion of having massive fast memory"*; worked `sum_array` example demonstrating both axes simultaneously (loop variables → [[TemporalLocality|temporal]]; consecutive array elements → [[SpatialLocality|spatial]] via the [[CacheLine|cache line]]); the **~5× row-major-vs-column-major matrix-sum benchmark** as the headline programmer payoff at zero algorithmic-complexity cost; desk/bookshelf/library analogy supplying [[WorkingSet|working-set]] intuition.
- **3 new concept pages minted:**
  - [[TemporalLocality]] — first locality axis; re-access the same item within a short time window; justifies caches / [[LeastRecentlyUsed|LRU]] replacement / register allocation.
  - [[SpatialLocality]] — second locality axis; access *nearby* items; exploited via [[CacheLine|cache-line]] block-granularity fetch; row-major-vs-column-major 5× speedup is the empirical signature.
  - [[WorkingSet]] — the *currently-active subset* of memory that locality keeps small; if working set ⊂ cache → cache-speed execution; if it overflows → thrashing.
- **1 in-place expansion:** [[LocalityOfReference]] gains DIS as a second source, an explicit DIS-quote pair for the two axes, and the **5× row-vs-column-major matrix benchmark** paragraph as the headline empirical demonstration.
- **1 entity update:** [[DiveIntoSystems]] — bumped from **107 → 108 chapters ingested**; Ch 11.3 connection paragraph prepended at top of Connections section.
- **No contradictions** — DIS's two-axis taxonomy matches the prior [[parproc-appA-systems-issues|ParProc App A]] treatment of [[LocalityOfReference]]; DIS adds code examples and the 5× speedup measurement not previously surfaced.
- **Validation:** all 4 new pages exist (`wiki/sources/dis-11-3-locality.md` + `wiki/concepts/{TemporalLocality,SpatialLocality,WorkingSet}.md`); all wikilinks within the new pages resolve to existing wiki pages or to each other; index `Sources` / `Concepts` sections updated; entity page [[DiveIntoSystems]] updated; this log entry appended.

## [2026-05-17] ingest | DSPy Optimizers

- Source: web URL `https://dspy.ai/learn/optimization/optimizers/` → fetched via WebFetch; raw markdown saved to `raw/dspy-optimizers.md`. **Page 13 of 13** of the [[DSPy]] *Learn* documentation and the **closing page of the corpus** — closes the DSPy *Learn* corpus at **13/13**.
- New source page: `wiki/sources/dspy-optimizers.md` — ships the **catalog** of specific optimizers deferred from [[dspy-optimization-overview|page 12]]. Documents eleven optimizers grouped into five families (Automatic Few-Shot Learning / Automatic Instruction Optimization / Automatic Finetuning / Program Transformations / Meta-Optimizers); the historical rename (*"Formerly called teleprompters"*); the three orthogonal what-it-tunes axes (demonstrations / instructions / LM weights); [[MIPROv2]]'s three-stage decomposition as the **structural template** for non-trivial optimizers (bootstrapping → grounded proposal → discrete search via [[BayesianOptimization|Bayesian Optimization]]); the **composability** claim — *"You can run `dspy.MIPROv2` and use the produced program as an input to `dspy.MIPROv2` again or, say, to `dspy.BootstrapFinetune` ... This is partly the essence of `dspy.BetterTogether`"*; the five-rule getting-started rubric (10 → [[BootstrapFewShot]], 50+ → [[BootstrapFewShotWithRandomSearch]], 0-shot → [[MIPROv2]] (0-shot mode), 40+ trials + 200+ examples → [[MIPROv2]] (full), post-success efficiency → [[BootstrapFinetune]]); DSPy's first explicit operating-cost ballpark ($2 typical, cents-to-tens-of-dollars range); three worked end-to-end optimization receipts (ReAct + MIPROv2(light) on HotPotQA 24%→51%; RAG + MIPROv2(medium) with `SemanticF1` on StackExchange 53%→61%; Banking77 classification + BootstrapFinetune on `gpt-4o-mini-2024-07-18` 66%→87% — the only weight-tuning receipt and the only `set_lm(...)` exercise in the corpus); plain-text-JSON save/load inspectability.
- **6 new concept pages minted:**
  - [[DSPyOptimizers]] — the canonical catalog-level page for DSPy's optimizer registry. **The catalog-level sibling of [[DSPyOptimization]]** (workflow vs catalog, mirroring the [[DSPyEvaluation]] / [[DSPyMetrics]] split). **Resolves the long-standing forward reference [[DSPyOptimizers]]** carried by every prior DSPy ingest since the corpus opened on 2026-05-17.
  - [[BootstrapFewShot]] — the canonical 10-example few-shot demonstration-tuning optimizer; recommended starting point for any optimization run with around 10 training examples; tunes only demonstrations via a `teacher`-bootstrap-then-metric-filter mechanism.
  - [[BootstrapFewShotWithRandomSearch]] — the random-search-augmented version of [[BootstrapFewShot]] for 50+ training examples; the canonical worked-example for the general optimizer API.
  - [[MIPROv2]] — the framework's **reference optimizer**; the only optimizer in the catalog that jointly tunes instructions and demonstrations in the same run; three-stage [[BayesianOptimization|Bayesian]]-search-driven algorithm whose decomposition is the structural template for non-trivial optimizers; used in two of the three worked receipts on page 13.
  - [[GEPA]] — the reflection-based instruction optimizer; the **only optimizer named on [[dspy-optimization-overview|page 12]]** — and named precisely because it is the **named carve-out** from the framework-level inverted 20/80 train/val split (*"the GEPA optimizer follows standard ML practice"*). Uses LM reflection on program trajectories + optional domain-specific textual feedback. **Resolves the long-standing forward reference [[GEPA]]** carried by [[DSPyOptimization]] / [[dspy-optimization-overview]] since page 12.
  - [[BootstrapFinetune]] — the **only weight-tuning optimizer** in the catalog; bridges DSPy into [[FineTuning]]; distills a prompt-based DSPy program into weight updates via supervised fine-tuning on metric-filtered bootstrapped traces. The recommended post-success efficiency move (prompt-optimize with a large LM, then BootstrapFinetune to distill into a smaller one). Banking77 worked receipt is the only weight-tuning receipt and the only `set_lm(...)` exercise in the corpus.
- Minor variants folded into the [[DSPyOptimizers]] catalog table rather than minted as separate pages: `KNNFewShot` / `COPRO` / `SIMBA` / `Ensemble` / `BetterTogether` / `LabeledFewShot` — each gets a row in the catalog table with its mechanism, parameters, and family.

- Contradictions: **none**. The Optimizers page **extends** every prior DSPy ingest by minting the catalog of specific algorithms the prior twelve pages deferred to it. The 20/80 split recommendation from [[dspy-optimization-overview|page 12]] is **operationalized** by this page's per-optimizer family structure — three of the four families are prompt optimizers (where the 20/80 split applies); [[BootstrapFinetune]] (the weight-tuning family) is implicitly outside the 20/80 recommendation since the *prompt-optimizers-overfit-on-small-training-sets* rationale doesn't apply; [[GEPA]] is the named exception from page 12 (uses conventional ML split). The page is **fully consistent** with the workflow-level commitments from page 12.
- One **productive new claim** the wiki captures for the first time: **DSPy optimizers are first-class composable units**. The Optimization stage's output is structurally identical to its input — a `dspy.Module` subclass — which makes *"run optimizer A, then run optimizer B on A's output"* a one-line operation. This is **structurally novel** relative to conventional ML hyperparameter optimization, where a `sklearn.GridSearchCV` doesn't typically consume another `GridSearchCV`'s output as input. DSPy can scale **both** *pre-inference-time compute* (the optimizer's budget) and *inference-time compute* (the ensemble's vote) from a single optimizer run — the `Ensemble` + top-k-extraction pattern operationalizes this directly. `BetterTogether` is what happens when this composability is **formalized as a sequence schedule** (prompt → weight → prompt).

- Source: [[dis-8-4-3-ia32-loops]] — `wiki/sources/dis-8-4-3-ia32-loops.md` (URL: https://diveintosystems.org/book/C8-IA32/loops.html). **Third and final leaf of [[dis-8-4-ia32-conditional-loops|Ch 8.4]]**; **83rd ingested DIS chapter**. **32-bit structural twin** of [[dis-7-4-3-x86-64-loops|Ch 7.4.3]]; closes the [[ControlFlow|control-flow]] section of Ch 8.
- Coverage: same `sumUp(int n)` worked example as Ch 7.4.3 at [[IA32]] width. Jump-to-test pattern rules carry over unchanged — (a) unconditional `jmp` to the condition, conditional jump on the un-negated condition branches **back** to the body (inverse of the [[dis-8-4-2-ia32-if-statements|Ch 8.4.2]] if-pattern); (b) `for`/`while` produce identical assembly; (c) `do`–`while` drops the leading `jmp`. IA32 deltas: stack-based locals at [[FramePointer|`%ebp`]]-relative negative offsets (`total` at `-0x8(%ebp)`, `i` at `-0x4(%ebp)`); parameter `n` read from `0x8(%ebp)` per [[CdeclCallingConvention|cdecl]]; 32-bit [[InstructionPointer|`%eip`]] jump targets; `leave; ret` epilogue (mnemonic vs `leaveq; retq`).

## [2026-05-17] ingest | Dive into Systems — Ch 8.4.2 If Statements in Assembly (IA32)

- Source: [[dis-8-4-2-ia32-if-statements]] — `wiki/sources/dis-8-4-2-ia32-if-statements.md` (URL: https://diveintosystems.org/book/C8-IA32/if_statements.html). **Second leaf of [[dis-8-4-ia32-conditional-loops|Ch 8.4]]**; **82nd ingested DIS chapter**. **32-bit structural twin** of [[dis-7-4-2-x86-64-if-statements|Ch 7.4.2]].
- Coverage: same `getSmallest(int x, int y)` worked example as Ch 7.4.2 at [[IA32]] width — traced through both branch-based and [[X86ConditionalMove|`cmov`]]-based forms. Branch-pattern rule unchanged (jump on the negated condition — `jle` fires when `x ≤ y`, falling through to the then-branch when `x > y` holds). `-O1` substitutes [[X86ConditionalMove|`cmovle`]] to eliminate the [[ControlHazard|control hazard]] / [[BranchPrediction|branch-prediction]] dependency, same safety constraint ([[NullPointerSafety|null-pointer dereference]] forces fallback). IA32 deltas: parameters read from stack via [[FramePointer|`%ebp`]]-anchored offsets (`mov 0x8(%ebp), %eax`; `cmp 0xc(%ebp), %eax`) per [[CdeclCallingConvention|cdecl]] vs `%edi`/`%esi` per [[SystemVCallingConvention|System V]]; return value in `%eax`; the `cmovle 0x8(%ebp), %eax` form takes a stack-memory source operand vs Ch 7.4.2's register-to-register `cmovle %edi, %eax`.
- New concept pages (0): reuses [[X86ConditionalMove]], [[AsmIfThenElse]], [[BranchInstruction]], [[CmpInstruction]], [[X86JumpInstructions]] from [[dis-7-4-2-x86-64-if-statements|Ch 7.4.2]] unchanged.

## [2026-05-17] ingest | Dive into Systems — Ch 8.4.1 Preliminaries (IA32 Conditional Control)

- Source: [[dis-8-4-1-ia32-preliminaries]] — `wiki/sources/dis-8-4-1-ia32-preliminaries.md` (URL: https://diveintosystems.org/book/C8-IA32/preliminaries.html). **First leaf of [[dis-8-4-ia32-conditional-loops|Ch 8.4]]**; **81st ingested DIS chapter**. **32-bit structural twin** of [[dis-7-4-1-x86-64-preliminaries|Ch 7.4.1]].
- Coverage: re-introduces the three mechanism families every conditional construct at the [[IA32]] [[AssemblyLanguage|assembly]] level rests on — [[X86FlagsRegister|FLAGS condition-code register]] (ZF / SF / OF / CF), comparison instructions [[CmpInstruction|`cmp`]] / [[TestInstruction|`test`]] (flag-only siblings of `sub` / `and`), and the full [[X86JumpInstructions|jump-instruction family]] (`jmp` unconditional + signed `je`/`jne`/`jg`/`jge`/`jl`/`jle` + unsigned `ja`/`jae`/`jb`/`jbe`). IA32 deltas: register-zero idiom is `test %eax, %eax` (not `test %rax, %rax`); jump targets are 32-bit [[InstructionPointer|`%eip`]]; FLAGS register itself is 32-bit EFLAGS but the four bits read (ZF/SF/OF/CF) are architecturally identical to RFLAGS. Signed/unsigned mnemonic split (`g`/`l` consume SF + OF; `a`/`b` consume CF) carries over unchanged — same [[TwosComplement|bit-pattern-interpretation-invariance]] at IA32 surface.
- New concept pages (0): reuses [[X86FlagsRegister]], [[ConditionCode]], [[CmpInstruction]], [[TestInstruction]], [[X86JumpInstructions]] from [[dis-7-4-1-x86-64-preliminaries|Ch 7.4.1]] unchanged.

## [2026-05-17] ingest | DSPy Metrics

- Source: web URL `https://dspy.ai/learn/evaluation/metrics/` → fetched via WebFetch; raw markdown saved to `raw/dspy-metrics.md`. **Page 11 of 13** of the [[DSPy]] *Learn* documentation; **third page of the [[DSPyEvaluation|Evaluation stage]]** (after [[dspy-evaluation-overview|the Evaluation Overview]] page 9 and [[dspy-data|Data Handling]] page 10).
- New source page: `wiki/sources/dspy-metrics.md` — expands Step 2 of [[DSPyEvaluation|the four-step iterative-evaluation loop]] (*"define a DSPy metric"*) into the concrete **`(example, pred, trace=None) -> score`** function-signature contract every DSPy artifact composes against. Documents the **dual-purpose `trace` argument** (continuous `float` return when `trace is None` for evaluation/optimization; strict `bool` return when `trace is not None` for bootstrap demo selection), the two regimes (simple scalar metrics vs AI-feedback / [[llmasjudge|LLM-as-judge]] metrics built from a `class Assess(dspy.Signature)` rubric + N `dspy.Predict(Assess)` invocations), the canonical hybrid pattern mixing AI-feedback with deterministic hard gates, the `validate_hops` trace-aware bootstrap recipe, the recursive-metric-optimization claim, and the `dspy.Evaluate` parallel dev-set-evaluation harness.
- **2 new concept pages minted:**
  - [[DSPyMetrics]] — the canonical concept page for the metric contract. **Resolves the long-standing forward reference [[DSPyMetrics]]** carried by [[DSPyEvaluation]] / [[dspy-evaluation-overview]] / [[dspy-data]] / [[DSPyExample]] / [[DSPyPrediction]] / [[DSPyModules]] / [[DSPyProgrammingModel]] / [[DSPy]] / every prior DSPy ingest since the corpus opened on 2026-05-17.
  - [[DSPyEvaluate]] — the canonical concept page for the `dspy.Evaluate` dev-set-evaluation utility (`from dspy.evaluate import Evaluate`); a reusable thread-parallel harness around the raw `for x in devset: pred = program(**x.inputs()); score = metric(x, pred)` loop with four kwargs (`devset` / `num_threads` / `display_progress` / `display_table`). Sibling to [[DSPyMetrics]] — a metric is the *what to measure*; `Evaluate` is the *how to apply the measurement*.
- **Re-used [[llmasjudge]] without duplicating** — the existing concept page is linked from both [[DSPyMetrics]] and [[dspy-metrics]] rather than minting a parallel `DSPyJudge` page. DSPy's AI-feedback metric is the structural operationalization of the general [[llmasjudge|LLM-as-judge]] pattern: the judge is a typed [[DSPySignatures|Signature]] (not a hand-written prompt) called through [[DSPyPredict|`dspy.Predict`]], adapted by an [[DSPyAdapters|Adapter]], routed through [[DSPyLM|`dspy.LM`]] — i.e. a fully DSPy-native artifact that composes with the rest of the framework.

The tweet receipt's `score = (correct + engaging) if correct and (len(tweet) <= 280) else 0` combines two LM-judged booleans with one deterministic boolean inside the same `score` computation. The page implies this **mixing** is the recommended shape — combine AI feedback with deterministic gates rather than going pure-LLM-judge. In [[LLMModuloFramework|LLM-Modulo]] terms: the deterministic gate is a *sound* critic; the LM-judged dimensions are *soft* critics. This is the wiki's first explicit DSPy receipt of the hybrid hard-gate-plus-soft-judge shape [[LLMModuloFramework|LLM-Modulo]] argues for.

## [2026-05-17] ingest | Dive into Systems — Ch 8.3 Additional Arithmetic Instructions (IA32)

- Source: [[dis-8-3-ia32-arithmetic]] — `wiki/sources/dis-8-3-ia32-arithmetic.md` (URL: https://diveintosystems.org/book/C8-IA32/arithmetic.html). **Third leaf of Ch 8 *32-bit IA32 Assembly***; **79th ingested DIS chapter**. **32-bit structural twin** of [[dis-7-3-x86-64-arithmetic|Ch 7.3]].
- Coverage: re-presents the extended-arithmetic surface ([[X86NegInstruction|`neg`]] / `inc` / `dec`, [[X86MulInstruction|`imul`]] / `mul`, [[X86DivInstruction|`idiv`]] / `div`, [[X86ShiftInstructions|`shl` / `sal` / `shr` / `sar`]], [[X86BitwiseInstructions|`and` / `or` / `xor` / `not`]], [[LeaInstruction|`lea`]]) at [[IA32]] 32-bit width. IA32 deltas: 32-bit (`l`) default operand width; [[X86MulInstruction|`mul` / `imul`]] one-operand form uses the **`%edx:%eax` pair** for the 64-bit product (high 32 in `%edx`, low 32 in `%eax`); [[X86DivInstruction|`idiv` / `div`]] reads dividend from `%edx:%eax`, quotient → `%eax`, remainder → `%edx` (`cltd` sign-extension idiom vs x86-64 `cqto`); shift count still in `%cl`. Headline rules carry over unchanged from Ch 7.3: `not` ≠ `neg` (`~x = -x - 1`); *shift-as-multiply* [[CompilerOptimization|strength reduction]]; [[LeaInstruction|`lea`]] as no-memory-access arithmetic shortcut.

## [2026-05-17] ingest | DSPy Modules

- Source: web URL `https://dspy.ai/learn/programming/modules/` → fetched via WebFetch; raw markdown saved to `raw/dspy-modules.md`. **Page 5 of 13** of the [[DSPy]] *Learn* documentation.
- New source page: `wiki/sources/dspy-modules.md` — **defines** the second of [[DSPyProgrammingModel|the Programming Model's]] four orthogonal artifacts; the pair-page to [[dspy-signatures]] (Signatures define the *what*, Modules define the *how*).
- **7 new concept pages minted:**
  - [[DSPyModules]] — the canonical concept page for the Module abstraction. **Resolves the long-standing forward reference** carried by [[DSPy]] / [[DSPyProgrammingModel]] / [[DSPySignatures]] / [[DSPyLM]] / [[dspy-learn-index]] / [[dspy-programming-overview]] / [[dspy-language-models]] / [[dspy-signatures]] since the corpus opened.
  - [[DSPyPredict]] — the minimal primitive Module; *"all other DSPy modules are built using `dspy.Predict`"*. The atomic unit of optimization in DSPy — every other Module is a wrapper over `dspy.Predict` plus a signature expansion.
  - [[DSPyProgramOfThought]] — output-code-then-execute strategy; the code-execution counterpart to [[ChainOfThought|CoT]]; built-in sound critic in the [[LLMModuloFramework|LLM-Modulo]] sense.
  - [[DSPyMultiChainComparison]] — N-CoT samples + an LM comparison Predict; strictly more expressive than [[DSPyMajority|`dspy.majority`]]'s syntactic vote at the cost of one extra LM call.
  - [[DSPyRecursiveLanguageModel]] — `dspy.RLM`, the **Recursive Language Model** for over-context cases; sandboxed Python REPL + recursive sub-LLM calls; DSPy's first-class context-overflow primitive.
  - [[DSPyMajority]] — function-style vote aggregator; **zero LM cost**; the only built-in that does not take a [[DSPySignatures|Signature]].
  - [[DSPyPrediction]] — the typed return-object every Module call produces; carries output fields as attributes + module-injected fields (e.g. `.reasoning`) + the `get_lm_usage()` cost-accounting telemetry (DSPy 2.6.16+).

## [2026-05-17] ingest | Dive into Systems — Ch 5.4.3 Storage Circuits

- Source: `wiki/sources/dis-5-4-3-storage-circuits.md` — [[SuzanneJMatthews|Matthews]] / [[TiaNewhall|Newhall]] / [[KevinCWebb|Webb]]'s **third and closing subsection** of [[dis-5-4-circuits|Ch 5.4]] delivering the **storage** category — **completes the three-category partition** ([[ArithmeticLogicCircuit|arithmetic-logic]] [[dis-5-4-1-arithmetic-logic-circuits|5.4.1]] / [[ControlCircuit|control]] [[dis-5-4-2-control-circuits|5.4.2]] / **storage 5.4.3**). URL: https://diveintosystems.org/book/C5-Arch/storagecircs.html.
- Headline thesis: *"A storage circuit's value depends on its input values and also its currently stored value"* — feedback loop is the structural signature distinguishing sequential storage from the combinational circuits of [[dis-5-4-1-arithmetic-logic-circuits|5.4.1]] / [[dis-5-4-2-control-circuits|5.4.2]].
- Two-technology split (foundational for the [[MemoryHierarchy|memory hierarchy]]): [[SRAM]] (circuit-based, fast, used for [[CpuRegister|registers]] / cache) vs [[DRAM]] (capacitor-based, denser but needs refresh, used for main [[RAM|memory]]).
- Three-layer gate-level build-up: [[SRLatch|**RS latch**]] (two cross-coupled [[NandGate|NAND]] gates with feedback; hold when `S=R=1`, set/reset on active-low pulses, forbidden `S=R=0`) → [[DLatch|**gated D latch**]] (adds [[WriteEnable|`WE`]] front-end; *"prevents simultaneous 0 inputs to both R and S"* — eliminates the forbidden case by construction) → **32-bit [[CpuRegister|register]]** (32 D-latches sharing one `WE`). The [[RegisterFile|register file]] (registers + [[Decoder|decoder]] + [[Multiplexer|MUX]]) is named conceptually but full wiring deferred.
- 9 new concept pages: [[StorageCircuit]] (**promoted from forward-reference to category page** — closes the three-category partition), [[Latch]] (generic 1-bit storage primitive — level-sensitive), [[FlipFlop]] (edge-triggered cousin — page documents the term as it surfaces and explicitly flags Ch 5.4.3's informal use as a [[DLatch|D-latch]] synonym + the omitted master-slave construction), [[SRLatch]] (NAND-based RS latch with the forbidden-input case), [[DLatch]] (gated D latch — the safe building block), [[WriteEnable]] (the control input; clock abstraction), [[ClockSignal]] (the elided wire — documents what the chapter abstracts away as forward-context), [[RegisterFile]] (registers + decoder + MUX), [[DRAM]] (capacitor-based main-memory technology — named only).
- In-place updates: [[CpuRegister]] gains a **gate-level construction section** ([[DLatch|D-latch]] / [[SRLatch|RS]] / [[WriteEnable|WE]] / [[SRAM]] connections) + adds [[dis-5-4-3-storage-circuits]] to sources frontmatter. [[DiveIntoSystems]] entity bumped 53→54 chapters with full chapter-description entry. `wiki/index.md` Sources entry added; "Added with [[dis-5-4-3-storage-circuits]]" block with all 9 new pages added above the [[dis-5-4-2-control-circuits|5.4.2]] block.
- Resolves the **long-standing forward-reference** to [[StorageCircuit]] that has been outstanding since [[dis-5-4-circuits|Ch 5.4]] hub and reaffirmed by [[dis-5-4-1-arithmetic-logic-circuits|5.4.1]] / [[dis-5-4-2-control-circuits|5.4.2]] siblings. No remaining DIS Ch 5.4 forward references.
- Scope notes (explicit chapter omissions, wiki-flagged): no edge-triggered master-slave [[FlipFlop|flip-flop]] construction; "flip-flop" used informally as a [[DLatch|D-latch]] synonym; no explicit [[ClockSignal|clock]] wire (abstracted to `WE`); no register-file decoder wiring built (named only); no DRAM 1T1C cell or refresh circuitry; no setup/hold or metastability treatment.
- Productive tension: [[FlipFlop]] page documents the **definitional gap** between the textbook's informal "flip-flop = D latch" usage and the wider digital-design vocabulary's edge-triggered semantics — flagged so the page can be promoted in-place when a later source builds a master-slave construction.
- Choice of [[SRAM]] over a new `StaticRAM` page: existing [[SRAM]] page (introduced by the embedded-systems corpora) already covers the technology; reused rather than duplicated. Symmetric [[DRAM]] page minted fresh.
- Validation: all `[[wikilinks]]` in the new pages resolve to existing pages — [[LogicGate]], [[NandGate]], [[Circuit]], [[StorageCircuit]] (newly minted), [[ArithmeticLogicCircuit]], [[ControlCircuit]], [[ArithmeticLogicUnit]], [[ControlUnit]], [[ProcessingUnit]], [[CpuRegister]], [[CPU]], [[FetchDecodeExecuteCycle]], [[MemoryHierarchy]], [[RAM]], [[SRAM]], [[Decoder]], [[Multiplexer]], [[Demultiplexer]], [[DataWord]], [[InstructionRegister]], [[ProgramCounter]], [[ControlBus]], [[AddressBus]], [[DataBus]], [[VonNeumannArchitecture]], [[DiveIntoSystems]], [[dis-5-2-von-neumann]], [[dis-5-3-gates]], [[dis-5-4-circuits]], [[dis-5-4-1-arithmetic-logic-circuits]], [[dis-5-4-2-control-circuits]] — plus the 9 new pages this ingest mints. **No forward references remain.**

## [2026-05-17] ingest | Dive into Systems — Ch 4.4.3 Multiplication and Division
- Source: `sources/dis-4-4-3-mult-div.md` ([[DiveIntoSystems]] Ch 4.4.3 — **third and final subsection** of [[dis-4-4-arithmetic|Ch 4.4 *Binary Integer Arithmetic*]]; **closes the [[BinaryArithmetic|binary integer arithmetic]] block** inside **Ch 4 *Binary and Data Representation***).
- URL: https://diveintosystems.org/book/C4-Binary/arithmetic_mult_div.html
- Authors: [[SuzanneJMatthews|Matthews]] / [[TiaNewhall|Newhall]] / [[KevinCWebb|Webb]].
- New concept pages: **2** — [[BinaryMultiplication]] (base-2 pencil-and-paper long multiplication — *the decimal long-multiplication algorithm transferred to base 2 with only the base changing from $10$ to $2$*; per-multiplier-bit partial-product rows each *either the multiplicand itself or zero* — binary's digit alphabet $\{0,1\}$ eliminates decimal's $9 \times 9$ multiplication-table requirement, reducing the operation to *select-or-zero, shift, and add*; each row shifted left by its bit position; final sum via [[BinaryAddition|Ch 4.4.1 binary addition]]; worked example $\mathtt{0b0101} \times \mathtt{0b0011}$ → $\mathtt{0b0101} + \mathtt{0b1010} = \mathtt{0b1111}$ — $5 \times 3 = 15$), [[BinaryDivision]] (base-2 pencil-and-paper long division; per-step decision reduces to a single magnitude comparison $R \ge B$ — quotient bit 1 and subtract, or quotient bit 0 and skip — eliminating decimal's $0$–$9$ digit-guess; trial-subtraction uses [[BinarySubtraction|Ch 4.4.2 subtraction]]; explicit restatement of **integer-division truncation** — *"in most languages (for example, C, Python 2, and Java) the fractional portion of the result gets truncated"* — supplying the binary-level mechanism behind the [[IntegerDivision|integer-division]] trap [[dis-1-1-getting-started|Ch 1.1]] introduced at the C-language level; `%` operator returns the integer remainder; worked example $\mathtt{1011}_2 \div \mathtt{11}_2$ → quotient $\mathtt{0011}_2 = 3$ remainder $\mathtt{10}_2 = 2$).
- Updated: [[DiveIntoSystems]] (corpus 40 → **41 chapters**; sources frontmatter list appended `dis-4-4-2-subtraction` + `dis-4-4-3-mult-div` — the prior ingest had omitted `dis-4-4-2-subtraction` from the frontmatter list, fixed in passing; Connections section added Ch 4.4.3 bullet; corpus-status paragraph extended with the Ch 4.4.3 summary).
- Index: added 1 source row above the [[dis-4-4-2-subtraction|Ch 4.4.2]] entry; added new `### Added with [[dis-4-4-3-mult-div]]` block in Concepts section with **2 new concept entries** ([[BinaryMultiplication]], [[BinaryDivision]]) inserted between [[dis-4-3-signed|Ch 4.3]] and [[dis-4-4-2-subtraction|Ch 4.4.2]] concept blocks.
- Overview: skipped per task scope.
- Key payoffs: (1) **closes the [[BinaryArithmetic|Ch 4.4 binary integer arithmetic]] three-subsection block** (Addition / Subtraction / Multiplication & Division) — Ch 4.4 → Ch 4.5 transition is now unblocked; (2) **instantiates the Ch 4.4 base-transfer thesis on the two remaining arithmetic operations**; (3) names the **simpler-than-decimal observation** for both — multiplication's per-digit choice collapses from $\{0,\ldots,9\}$ to $\{0,1\}$ (no multiplication table), division's per-step choice collapses from "guess a digit $0$–$9$" to "compare $R \ge B$" (single magnitude check); (4) **extends the hardware-reuse theme** — addition (4.4.1) / subtraction (4.4.2) / multiplication (partial-product summation) / division (trial-subtraction) all share the same $N$-bit adder core, making the [[dis-4-4-arithmetic|Ch 4.4]] *unified-ALU* preview concrete on all four arithmetic operations; (5) supplies the **binary-level mechanism** behind [[IntegerDivision|integer-division truncation]] — the trap [[dis-1-1-getting-started|Ch 1.1]] introduced at the C-language level — making the cross-chapter link Ch 1.1 ↔ Ch 4.4.3 explicit; (6) names the **`%` operator's binary mechanism** — long division naturally produces a separate integer remainder; modulus just returns that remainder.
- Explicit scope disclaimers: Ch 4.4.3 opens with *"these methods do not reflect the behavior of modern hardware and are not meant to be comprehensive"*. **Out of scope** (flagged as forward references on the new pages): (a) hardware multipliers (Booth / Wallace / array); (b) hardware dividers (restoring / non-restoring / SRT); (c) the **[[BitShift|bit-shift]] power-of-two shortcut** (×$2^k$ = left-shift $k$; ÷$2^k$ = right-shift $k$ with [[SignExtension|sign extension]] for signed); (d) signed-multiplication sign handling; (e) signed-division remainder-sign conventions (C99 truncate-toward-zero vs. Python 3 floor-div); (f) division-by-zero trap behaviour; (g) multiplication overflow rules.
- BitShift decision: per task instructions (*"BitShift (if covered as multiplication-by-2 shortcut). Only new."*), **[[BitShift]] is NOT created** — Ch 4.4.3 does not cover bit shifting as a power-of-two shortcut. It uses left-shift only as part of the long-multiplication partial-product layout (a structural piece of the algorithm, not a standalone optimization). [[BitShift]] retained as a *forward reference* on both new concept pages so a future hardware-realistic treatment can promote it from stub to first-class concept.
- Validation: new source page [[wikilinks]] resolve to existing pages ([[DiveIntoSystems]] / [[SuzanneJMatthews]] / [[TiaNewhall]] / [[KevinCWebb]] / [[BinaryNumber]] / [[UnsignedInteger]] / [[TwosComplement]] / [[IntegerDivision]] / [[CArithmeticOperators]] / [[BinaryAddition]] / [[BinarySubtraction]] / [[SignExtension]] / [[dis-4-1-bases]] / [[dis-4-2-conversion]] / [[dis-4-3-signed]] / [[dis-4-4-arithmetic]] / [[dis-4-4-1-addition]] / [[dis-4-4-2-subtraction]] / [[dis-1-1-getting-started]]) plus 2 new pages just created ([[BinaryMultiplication]] / [[BinaryDivision]]). Forward references [[BitShift]] / [[BinaryArithmetic]] / [[FullAdder]] / [[IntegerOverflow]] / [[ArithmeticLogicUnit]] / [[XOR]] used as not-yet-promoted stubs — [[BitShift]] intentionally left uncreated per the *only-if-covered* task constraint.
- Contradictions: none — purely additive. Ch 4.4.3 reuses [[BinaryAddition|Ch 4.4.1 addition]] for partial-product summation and [[BinarySubtraction|Ch 4.4.2 subtraction]] for division's trial-subtraction, extending [[dis-4-4-arithmetic|Ch 4.4]]'s hardware-reuse theme without revising any prior claim.

## [2026-05-17] ingest | Dive into Systems — Ch 4.4.2 Subtraction
- Source: `sources/dis-4-4-2-subtraction.md` ([[DiveIntoSystems]] Ch 4.4.2 — **second subsection** of [[dis-4-4-arithmetic|Ch 4.4 *Binary Integer Arithmetic*]] inside **Ch 4 *Binary and Data Representation***).
- URL: https://diveintosystems.org/book/C4-Binary/arithmetic_subtraction.html
- Authors: [[SuzanneJMatthews|Matthews]] / [[TiaNewhall|Newhall]] / [[KevinCWebb|Webb]].
- New concept pages: **1** — [[BinarySubtraction]] (the base-2 subtraction operation implemented as **addition of the [[TwosComplement|two's-complement]] negation** $A + (-B)$; reuses the [[dis-4-4-1-addition|Ch 4.4.1]] [[BinaryAddition|adder]] via a row of [[XOR|XOR]] gates on operand B (the *flip-all-bits* step) plus [[CarryIn|`CarryIn`]] = 1 on the LSB adder (the *add one* step), folding the [[TwosComplement|two's-complement]] negation into the addition at **zero extra arithmetic hardware**; mode-switching unifies to one wire `Subtract`; worked examples $7 - 3 = 4$ and $7 - (-3) = 10$ verify all four sign combinations work via the same circuit — the [[dis-4-4-arithmetic|Ch 4.4]] interpretation-invariance reapplied; MSB carry-out *"doesn't necessarily indicate overflow"* in subtraction mode — full [[IntegerOverflow|overflow]] rule deferred).
- Updated: [[DiveIntoSystems]] (corpus 39 → **40 chapters**; Connections section added Ch 4.4.2 bullet above Ch 4.4.1; corpus-status paragraph extended). Skipped overview per task scope.
- Index: added 1 source row above the [[dis-4-4-1-addition|Ch 4.4.1]] entry; added an `### Added with [[dis-4-4-2-subtraction]]` concept block with the 1 new page above the Ch 4.4.1 concept block.
- Key payoffs: (1) cashes in [[dis-4-4-1-addition|Ch 4.4.1]]'s `CarryIn` hook — `CarryIn` was hard-wired to `0` for ordinary addition but exists architecturally *because of* subtraction; this chapter delivers on that promise. (2) the **add/subtract unit** mode-switches on a single `Subtract` wire: `Subtract = 0` → ordinary addition ($A + B$); `Subtract = 1` → XOR-row flips B and LSB `CarryIn = 1` → $A + (\overline{B} + 1) = A - B$. (3) **sign-combination universality** — the same circuit handles $A - B$ for all four sign combinations (`+ − +`, `+ − −`, `− − +`, `− − −`) without special-case logic, reapplying [[dis-4-4-arithmetic|Ch 4.4]] interpretation-invariance to the subtraction case. (4) **MSB carry-out semantics shift** between addition and subtraction modes — anticipates the [[IntegerOverflow|overflow]] story (worked example $7 - (-3) = \mathtt{0b1010}$ has MSB=1 but is the correct unsigned $10$).
- Validation: new source-page [[wikilinks]] resolve to existing pages ([[DiveIntoSystems]] / [[SuzanneJMatthews]] / [[TiaNewhall]] / [[KevinCWebb]] / [[TwosComplement]] / [[BinaryAddition]] / [[Carry]] / [[CarryIn]] / [[CarryOut]] / [[UnsignedInteger]] / [[MostSignificantBit]] / [[dis-4-1-bases]] / [[dis-4-3-signed]] / [[dis-4-4-arithmetic]] / [[dis-4-4-1-addition]]) and to the 1 new concept page ([[BinarySubtraction]]). Forward references [[FullAdder]] / [[ArithmeticLogicUnit]] / [[BinaryArithmetic]] / [[IntegerOverflow]] / [[XOR]] / [[DigitalLogic]] / [[ComputerArchitecture]] used as not-yet-promoted stubs — awaiting future [[dis-4-4-3-multiplication-division|Ch 4.4.3]] ingest and the [[DigitalLogic|digital-logic]] block.
- Contradictions: none — purely additive. Reuses [[dis-4-3-signed|Ch 4.3]]'s [[TwosComplement|two's-complement]] negation recipe and [[dis-4-4-1-addition|Ch 4.4.1]]'s [[BinaryAddition|adder]] machinery without revision; refines [[dis-4-4-1-addition|Ch 4.4.1]]'s *"hardware simply drops or truncates"* MSB-carry-out rule by noting the truncated bit's **meaning** depends on the addition vs. subtraction mode.

## [2026-05-17] ingest | ParProcBook Appendix B: Review of Matrix Algebra

- Source page created: `wiki/sources/parproc-appB-matrix-algebra.md` — Appendix B (pp. 313–320) of *Programming on Parallel Machines* by [[NormMatloff]]. Eight-section math primer: §B.1 notation + [[MatrixMultiplication|matrix multiplication]] (inner product definition, non-commutativity); §B.2 [[MatrixTranspose|transpose]] ($(AB)' = B'A'$, orthogonal matrices $U^{-1}=U'$); §B.3 [[LinearIndependence|linear independence]]; §B.4 [[Determinant|determinants]] (recursive cofactor expansion, base case $sv-tu$); §B.5 [[MatrixInversion|matrix inverse]] (identity matrix, invertibility conditions, $(AB)^{-1}=B^{-1}A^{-1}$, QR decomposition route); §B.6 [[Eigenvalue|eigenvalues]] and [[Eigenvector|eigenvectors]] ($AX=\lambda X$, diagonalizability conditions for symmetric and distinct-eigenvalue cases); §B.7 [[Rank|rank]] ($\text{rk}(A')=\text{rk}(A)$, $\text{rk}(A)\leq\min(r,s)$, $\text{rk}(A'A)=\text{rk}(A)$); §B.8 R syntax (`%*%`, `t()`, `solve()`, `eigen()`, `qr()`, `qr.solve()`, `diag()`).
- 2 new concept pages: [[MatrixTranspose]], [[LinearIndependence]].
- Extended existing pages: [[Determinant]] (§B.4 recursive formula section + connections), [[Rank]] (expanded from stub — §B.7 formal properties + R syntax + connections), [[Eigenvalue]] (§B.6 diagonalizability conditions + R syntax + footnote on SVD), [[Eigenvector]] (§B.6 orthogonality for symmetric case), [[MatrixInversion]] (§B.5 definitions + QR route + R syntax + connections).
- Updated: `wiki/index.md` (source row for parproc-appB, 2 new concept rows, count 299→300), `wiki/log.md` (this entry).
- Contradictions: None. The Ch11 footnote calling singular values "synonyms" for eigenvalues is refined (not contradicted) by the Appendix B footnote directing nonsquare cases to SVD.

- Updated [[NoFreeLunchTheorem]] context (Murphy §1.2.4 is the canonical reference) without rewriting the page.
- Overview gains **Corpus VI** — the theory-and-foundations textbook layer the other corpora tacitly assume. Re-anchors Corpus II's NLL/MLE/ERM prerequisites; sits between Corpus I (math) and Corpus III (interpretability).
- Sampled chapters: Preface, Ch 1 (Introduction — supervised/unsupervised/RL, ERM, MLE, NLL, overfitting, no-free-lunch, datasets, discussion incl. reward hacking + IRL). Structural understanding via brief contents (5 parts, 23 chapters + Notation appendix). Remaining 800+ pages not read in full.

## [2026-05-16] ingest | Mathematics for Machine Learning

- *Mathematics for Machine Learning* by [[MarcDeisenroth|Marc Peter Deisenroth]], [[AAldoFaisal|A. Aldo Faisal]] & [[ChengSoonOng|Cheng Soon Ong]] ([[CambridgeUniversityPress]] 2020; draft revision 2024-01-15; freely available at <https://mml-book.com>). 417 pages, 12 chapters across Part I (Mathematical Foundations) + Part II (Central Machine Learning Problems).
- Source page: [[mml-book]].
- **New entities (6):** [[MarcDeisenroth]], [[AAldoFaisal]], [[ChengSoonOng]], [[CambridgeUniversityPress]], [[ImperialCollegeLondon]], [[CSIROsData61]].
- **New concepts (~33):** [[Abduction]], [[BayesianLinearRegression]], [[CauchySchwarzInequality]], [[CholeskyDecomposition]], [[ConjugatePrior]], [[ConvexOptimization]], [[DataCovarianceMatrix]], [[DesignMatrix]], [[Determinant]], [[Eigendecomposition]], [[EMAlgorithm]], [[ExponentialFamily]], [[FeatureMap]], [[GaussianDistribution]], [[GaussianMixtureModel]], [[InnerProduct]], [[Jacobian]], [[KernelTrick]], [[LagrangeMultipliers]], [[LinearMapping]], [[MAPEstimation]], [[Margin]], [[MatrixDecomposition]], [[MixtureModel]], [[ModelSelection]], [[Norm]], [[OrthogonalProjection]], [[PartialDerivative]], [[ProbabilitySpace]], [[RandomVariable]], [[Responsibility]], [[SeparatingHyperplane]], [[SingularValueDecomposition]], [[SymmetricPositiveDefiniteMatrix]], [[Trace]], [[VectorSpace]].
- **Updated existing pages (12):** added `mml-book` as co-source on [[EmpiricalRiskMinimization]], [[MaximumLikelihoodEstimation]], [[CrossValidation]], [[PrincipalComponentAnalysis]], [[SupportVectorMachine]], [[GradientDescent]], [[Backpropagation]], [[CrossEntropyLoss]], [[Gradient]]; expanded stubs [[taylorseries]] (now a full page on multivariate Taylor / Linearization) and [[lagrangetheorem]] (now a disambiguation page pointing to [[LagrangeMultipliers]]).
- Overview gains **Corpus VII** — the prerequisites-and-foundations layer that Murphy/Bishop/MacKay tacitly assume. MML pairs naturally with [[pml1-murphy]]: MML is "math-first, four-ML-examples-deep"; Murphy is "probability-first, encyclopedic." The two corpora cross-reference on every overlapping chapter ([[EmpiricalRiskMinimization]], [[MaximumLikelihoodEstimation]], optimization, PCA, regression).
- Sampled material: front matter (foreword, target-audience taxonomy, symbol table); Ch 1 (intro/motivation/roadmap, full); openers of Ch 2 (Linear Algebra), Ch 3 (Analytic Geometry), Ch 4 (Matrix Decompositions), Ch 5 (Vector Calculus), Ch 6 (Probability), Ch 7 (Optimization), Ch 8 (When Models Meet Data — full opener through ERM), Ch 9 (Linear Regression — through MLE closed form + polynomial features), Ch 10 (PCA — through M-dim subspace derivation), Ch 11 (GMM — through EM mean updates), Ch 12 (SVM — through hard-margin primal). Remaining ~250 pages of detailed proofs/exercises relied on TOC + chapter mind maps; left as future selective sub-ingests.
- Contradictions / notational quirks: (i) MML uses **row-vector** convention for the gradient (§5.40), differing from Murphy/Goodfellow's column-vector convention — flagged on [[Gradient]] and [[Jacobian]]; (ii) MML defines linear regression *without bias* (Eq. 9.4), requiring the $x^{(0)}=1$ augmentation for an intercept ([[DesignMatrix]] page documents this); (iii) MML's framing is *agnostic* on the monolithic-vs-Agentic-AI question — [[2605.12966-agentic-ai-to-agi]]'s [[AverageTrap]] critique reads MML's structural choices as implicit acceptance of monolithic scaling, a tension Murphy shares.

- *Dive into Deep Learning* (D2L) — Installation chapter. ## [2026-05-16] ingest | D2L Notation

- *Dive into Deep Learning* (D2L) — Notation chapter. Reference symbol table that fixes typographic conventions across five domains used throughout the book: numerical objects (scalar $x$ / vector $\mathbf{x}$ / matrix $\mathbf{X}$ / [[Tensor|tensor $\mathsf{X}$]]), set theory, functions / operators ([[Norm|norm]], [[InnerProduct|inner product]], transpose, Hadamard), calculus ([[PartialDerivative|partial derivative]], [[Gradient|gradient $\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}$]]), and probability / information theory ([[RandomVariable|$X$]], $P(X\mid Y)$, $\mathbb{E}[X]$, variance / covariance / Pearson $\rho$, entropy $H(X)$, KL-divergence $D_{\textrm{KL}}$).
- Source page: [[d2l-notation]].
- New entities (0): none — pure notation reference, no new actors.
- New concepts (0): deliberately did **not** create stub pages for every math primitive (scalar, vector, matrix, set, indicator, transpose, Hadamard, summation, …) — would bloat the wiki and these are universally known. Source page wikilinks reuse existing concept pages: [[Tensor]], [[Norm]], [[InnerProduct]], [[Gradient]], [[PartialDerivative]], [[RandomVariable]], [[ProbabilitySpace]].
- Updated existing pages: none (existing concept pages already cite better primary sources — e.g. [[Gradient]] / [[Norm]] cite [[mml-book]]; [[Tensor]] cites [[madewithml-baselines]] — adding `d2l-notation` to those frontmatters would be noise since this chapter only fixes *symbols*, not definitions).
- Overview: not modified — notation chapter is meta / typographic and does not warrant a new corpus entry or revision.
- Contradictions: none direct. **Notational caveat** noted on the source page: [[mml-book]] uses a row-vector gradient convention; D2L's notation chapter is silent on layout, so the two are not yet in conflict — revisit if/when a later D2L chapter commits to a layout explicitly.
- Index: source count bumped 217 → 218; entities unchanged at 140; concepts unchanged at 391.

## [2026-05-16] ingest | D2L Introduction

- *Dive into Deep Learning* (D2L) — Introduction chapter (`raw/d2l-en/chapter_introduction/`, 1879 lines). First substantive chapter of D2L: broad survey of [[MachineLearning]] and [[DeepLearning]]. Defines ML as "the study of algorithms that can learn from experience"; introduces the **four-core-components** framing (data, model, objective, optimizer); catalogs **four paradigms** ([[SupervisedLearning]] — [[Regression]], [[Classification]], tagging, search, [[RecommenderSystems]], sequence learning; [[UnsupervisedLearning]]; [[SelfSupervisedLearning]]; [[reinforcementlearning|RL]] with [[MarkovDecisionProcess|MDP]] / contextual-bandit / [[MultiArmedBandits]] cases); traces historical roots from Bernoulli / Gauss / Fisher / Shannon / Turing / Hebb / Rosenblatt through the 1995–2005 NN winter to the [[DeepLearning]] revival; defines deep learning as multi-level [[RepresentationLearning]] with [[EndToEndTraining|end-to-end training]] replacing handcrafted [[FeatureEngineering|feature engineering]].
- Source page: [[d2l-introduction]].
- New entities (3): [[ImageNet]] (the 28%→2.25% top-5-error benchmark), [[MNIST]] ("considered huge" in the pre-DL era), [[Tesla]] (partial-autonomy self-driving — DL primarily on perception).
- New concepts (10): [[MachineLearning]], [[SelfSupervisedLearning]], [[Regression]], [[Classification]], [[EndToEndTraining]], [[RepresentationLearning]], [[RecommenderSystems]], [[MarkovDecisionProcess]], [[generativeadversarialnetwork|GAN]], [[DiffusionModel]].
- Updated existing pages: [[DeepLearning]] (substantial expansion — was a stub; now the corpus's anchor page for DL framing per D2L), [[SupervisedLearning]] (added D2L source + sub-task taxonomy), [[UnsupervisedLearning]] (added D2L source + five archetypal questions), [[reinforcementlearning]] (added D2L source + RL hierarchy + landmark deep-RL results), [[ComputerVision]] (pre-DL → DL transition + ImageNet numbers), [[NLP]] (NLP-task survey), [[SpeechRecognition]] (sequence-to-sequence + Siri/Alexa/Google framing). Deliberately did *not* create stubs for every chapter mention — kept Fisher / Hinton / LeCun / Bengio / Schmidhuber / Hebb / Rosenblatt / Caffe / Theano / Chainer as red wikilinks where they appear in the source page, since the chapter only mentions them in passing.
- Overview: **bumped corpus count 8 → 9** and added a new section "IX. *Dive into Deep Learning*" covering all four D2L sources ingested so far. Top-of-overview synthesis paragraph updated.
- Contradictions: none direct. Mild framing tension with [[2605.12966-agentic-ai-to-agi]]'s [[AverageTrap]] — D2L's chapter implicitly endorses monolithic supervised learning at scale. Flagged on [[d2l-introduction]].
- Index: source count bumped 218 → 219; entities bumped 140 → 143; concepts bumped 391 → 401.

## [2026-05-16] ingest | D2L Attention and Transformers
- Source: `wiki/sources/d2l-attention-and-transformers.md` — 9-section [[Attention|attention]] + [[Transformer|Transformer]] chapter (`raw/d2l-en/chapter_attention-mechanisms-and-transformers/`: queries-keys-values / attention-pooling / attention-scoring-functions / bahdanau-attention / multihead-attention / self-attention-and-positional-encoding / transformer / large-pretraining-transformers / vision-transformer). The pivot from gated-RNN seq2seq into the Transformer regime. Builds attention from the [[QueryKeyValue|database query]] analogy up: [[NadarayaWatson|Nadaraya–Watson]] kernel regression (1964) as half-century precursor → [[ScaledDotProductAttention|scaled dot-product]] vs [[AdditiveAttention|additive]] [[AttentionScoringFunctions|scoring]] with [[MaskedSoftmax|masked softmax]] for padding/causal masks → [[BahdanauAttention|Bahdanau, Cho & Bengio 2014]] differentiable alignment ($\mathbf{c}_{t'}=\sum_t\alpha(\mathbf{s}_{t'-1},\mathbf{h}_t)\mathbf{h}_t$) as fix for [[1409.3215-seq2seq]]'s fixed-vector bottleneck → [[MultiHeadAttention]] as projection-diversified parallel pools → [[SelfAttention]] (the all-pairs view: $O(n^2 d)$ compute, $O(1)$ sequential ops, $O(1)$ max path length vs CNN/RNN) + sinusoidal [[PositionalEncoding]] with the $2\times 2$ relative-offset projection identity → the [[Transformer]] block ([[1706.03762-attention-is-all-you-need|Vaswani et al. 2017]]): multi-head + positionwise [[FeedForwardNetwork|FFN]] + [[ResidualConnection|residual]] + [[LayerNormalization|LayerNorm]] post-norm, decoder adds causal-masked self-attention + encoder–decoder cross-attention → the three pretraining modes — encoder-only [[BERT]] / encoder–decoder [[T5]] / decoder-only [[GPT]] family — with [[2001.08361-scaling-laws|Kaplan et al.]] power-law scaling, [[InContextLearning|in-context learning]], [[ChainOfThought|CoT]], [[RLHF]] / [[InstructGPT]] → and the [[VisionTransformer|Vision Transformer]] ([[Dosovitskiy|Dosovitskiy]] et al. 2021): patches-as-tokens (linear projection ≡ stride-$p$ conv) + learnable [[ClsToken|`<cls>` token]] + learned positional embeddings + [[PreNorm|pre-norm]] + [[GELU]] MLP — "scalability trumps inductive biases."
- New entities (1): [[Dosovitskiy]] (ViT lead author 2021).

## [2026-05-16] ingest | D2L Optimization
- Source: `wiki/sources/d2l-optimization.md` — 11-section *Optimization Algorithms* chapter (`raw/d2l-en/chapter_optimization/`: optimization-intro / convexity / gd / sgd / minibatch-sgd / momentum / adagrad / rmsprop / adadelta / adam / lr-scheduler) — D2L's deepest optimization-theory excursion. Optimization-vs-generalization framing; nonconvex pathologies (local minima, [[SaddlePoint|saddle points]] exponentially dominant in high $d$, [[VanishingGradient|vanishing gradients]]); [[Convexity]] primer (sets / functions / Jensen / Hessian-PSD / Lagrangian / penalty / projection — reinterpreting [[WeightDecay]] as penalty and [[GradientClipping]] as $\ell_2$-ball projection); [[GradientDescent]] from Taylor expansion + [[NewtonsMethod]] + [[Preconditioning|diagonal preconditioning]] + [[LineSearch]]; [[StochasticGradientDescent|SGD]] with $\mathcal{O}(1/\sqrt{T})$ convex convergence (Nesterov-Vial 2000); [[MinibatchSGD]] (variance ↓ $b^{-1/2}$); [[Momentum|momentum]] eigendecomposition analysis on convex quadratics ([[BorisPolyak|Polyak]] 1964, [[YuriiNesterov|Nesterov]] 2018); [[Adagrad]] ([[JohnDuchi|Duchi]], Hazan & Singer 2011) → [[RMSProp]] ([[GeoffreyHinton|Tieleman & Hinton]] 2012) → [[Adadelta]] ([[MatthewZeiler|Zeiler]] 2012) → [[Adam]] ([[DiederikKingma|Kingma]] & [[JimmyBa|Ba]] 2014) progression with **bias correction** $\hat{\mathbf{v}}_t/(1-\beta_1^t)$, $\hat{\mathbf{s}}_t/(1-\beta_2^t)$; [[Yogi]] divergence fix; [[LearningRateScheduler|LR scheduler]] taxonomy (magnitude / decay / [[Warmup|warmup]]) with canonical policies (factor / multifactor / polynomial / [[CosineLRSchedule|cosine annealing]] Loshchilov & Hutter 2016 / linear warmup Gotmare et al. 2018).
- New entities (6): [[DiederikKingma]] (Adam co-author 2014; VAE 2013); [[JimmyBa]] (Adam co-author 2014; LayerNorm 2016); [[BorisPolyak]] (heavy-ball momentum 1964); [[YuriiNesterov]] (accelerated gradient 1983, *Lectures on Convex Optimization* 2018); [[JohnDuchi]] (Adagrad lead author 2011, Stanford statistics); [[MatthewZeiler]] (Adadelta 2012, Clarifai founder).
- New concepts (11): [[Adagrad]], [[RMSProp]], [[Adadelta]], [[Convexity]], [[SaddlePoint]], [[NewtonsMethod]], [[Preconditioning]], [[NesterovMomentum]], [[CosineLRSchedule]], [[Warmup]], [[Yogi]].
- Updated existing pages (substantive expansion): [[Adam]] (one-liner stub → full bias-correction algorithm + position in family + AdamW vs Adam + Yogi divergence-mode note); [[Momentum]] (one-liner stub → full Polyak heavy-ball + recursive expansion + effective sample weight $\frac{1}{1-\beta}$ + eigendecomposition analysis + author history); [[LearningRateScheduler]] (one-liner stub → three-axes-of-scheduling table + canonical policies + modern default recipes + framework support). Frontmatter `sources:` field bumped on [[GradientDescent]], [[StochasticGradientDescent]], [[MinibatchSGD]], [[LearningRate]], [[VanishingGradient]], [[ConvexOptimization]], [[Hessian]], and [[GeoffreyHinton]] (added RMSProp co-introducer note to the latter's body).
- Overview: §IX bumped 14 → 15 chapters; appended a substantial d2l-optimization summary line covering the optimization-vs-generalization framing, nonconvex pathologies, the convexity primer, the algorithm tour (GD → Newton → SGD → minibatch → momentum), the Adagrad → RMSProp → Adadelta → Adam progression, Yogi, and the warmup + cosine LR-schedule recipe that anchors LLM pretraining.

- 12 key claims covering: the MDP tuple $(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},T,r)$ with transition kernel / user-designed reward / discount factor $\gamma$; the Markov property and state-expansion trick (location + velocity); stochastic vs deterministic policies; $V^\pi$ and $Q^\pi$ definitions; the Bellman one-step decomposition as the foundation of all RL; Bellman's principle of dynamic programming ("remainder of an optimal trajectory is also optimal"); Value Iteration as the fixed-point iteration converging from any $V_0$ to $V^*$ in ~10 sweeps on FrozenLake-v1; Value Iteration requires full MDP knowledge; Q-Learning ([[ChristopherWatkins|Watkins]] & Dayan 1992) as model-free Value Iteration with sampled transitions and a TD update; off-policy structure (behavior $\pi_e$ vs target $\arg\max\hat{Q}$); $\epsilon$-greedy + softmax-temperature exploration; the self-correcting overestimation property; the ~10 vs ~250 sample-efficiency gap as the price of model-free learning. 5 key quotes (Markov state-expansion, Bellman decomposition power, "remainder of an optimal trajectory" mnemonic, Q-Learning's "subvert the need to know the transition function" insight, RL's "collect the right kind of data" distinction from supervised learning).

## [2026-05-16] ingest | D2L Gaussian Processes
- Source: `raw/d2l-en/chapter_gaussian-processes/` (4 `.md` sections: index, gp-intro, gp-priors, gp-inference) → [[d2l-gaussian-processes|sources/d2l-gaussian-processes.md]]. Guest three-section chapter by [[AndrewGordonWilson|Wilson]] ([[NewYorkUniversity|NYU]] / [[amazon|Amazon]]) — D2L's only function-space Bayesian chapter, an outlier in the otherwise-frequentist-supervised-deep-learning corpus.
- 12 key claims covering: GP definition (collection of RVs, any finite subset multivariate Gaussian); two-moment specification (mean function + kernel); weight-space ⇄ function-space equivalence with $f(x)=w^\top\phi(x)$, $w\sim\mathcal{N}(u,S)$; the [[RBFKernel|RBF kernel]] derived as the $J\to\infty$ Riemann-sum limit of $J$ Gaussian basis functions (universal approximator with finite computation, infinite parameters); the [[NeuralNetworkKernel|NN kernel]] from infinite-width Bayesian NN ([[RadfordNeal|Neal 1996]] → [[NeuralTangentKernel|NTK]] precursor); stationary vs non-stationary kernel distinction; closed-form Gaussian posterior under additive Gaussian noise; [[MarginalLikelihood|marginal-likelihood]] hyperparameter learning with data-fit + log-det complexity decomposition encoding [[OccamsRazor|Occam's razor]]; epistemic / aleatoric uncertainty decomposition; $\mathcal{O}(n^3)$ Cholesky bottleneck and scalable-inference response ([[GPyTorch]]); predictive mean as a linear smoother; ~10k training-point cap on exact GPs. 5 key quotes (GP definition, "everything is a special case of a GP" joke, RBF-GP as universal approximator, exact closed-form Bayesian inference, "uncertainty is meaningless without precision").
- New entity pages (3): [[AndrewGordonWilson]] (NYU / Amazon; chapter author; GPyTorch co-author), [[NewYorkUniversity]] (Wilson + Bowman's home institution), [[GPyTorch]] (PyTorch-integrated scalable-GP library — Gardner et al. 2018).

Reused: [[BayesianLinearRegression]] (existing, sources bumped + GP-link section expanded), [[NeuralTangentKernel]] (existing, sources bumped + cross-link to [[NeuralNetworkKernel]] added), [[GaussianDistribution]] / [[CholeskyDecomposition]] / [[KernelTrick]] / [[BayesianOptimization]] / [[NadarayaWatson]] (already existed). Not created (be-judicious / forward-pointing): InducingPoint, SKI / KISS-GP, SpectralMixtureKernel, OUKernel, BayesianDeepLearning, ActiveLearning, OccamsRazor, Stationarity, UniversalApproximator, Hyperparameter, LengthScale, Amplitude, GaussianNoise, JacobGardner, RadfordNeal, MacKay2003 — flagged as stub-promotion candidates for any future Bayesian / kernel-methods ingest.

## [2026-05-16] ingest | D2L Hyperparameter Optimization
- Source: `raw/d2l-en/chapter_hyperparameter-optimization/` (5 `.md` sections: index, hyperopt-intro, hyperopt-api, rs-async, sh-intro, sh-async) → [[d2l-hyperparameter-optimization|sources/d2l-hyperparameter-optimization.md]]. Guest five-section chapter by [[AaronKlein|Klein]], [[MatthiasSeeger|Seeger]] & [[CedricArchambeau|Archambeau]] ([[Amazon]]) — D2L's only chapter cast entirely as black-box optimization.
- 12 key claims covering: HPO as black-box global optimization with no usable gradient + noisy / expensive evaluations; structured / conditional config spaces with per-hyperparameter priors (log-uniform for orders-of-magnitude ranges); random search as the universal baseline; the Bergstra-Bengio 2012 random-beats-grid result on effective-low-dim problems; the two random-search shortcomings (non-adaptive + uniform resource) that motivate model-based + multi-fidelity methods; the `HPOSearcher` / `HPOScheduler` / `HPOTuner` decomposition mirrored by SyneTune / Ray Tune / Optuna; Snoek-2012-style Bayesian optimization dominating random search after ~1000 s wall-clock; async parallel random search achieving linear $K$-worker speed-up; multi-fidelity HPO via $f(\mathbf{x}, r)$ reducing *total compute* (not just wall-clock); Successive Halving (Jamieson-Talwalkar 2016 / Karnin et al. 2013) with $N=\eta^K$ configs and rung set $\{r_\text{min}, r_\text{min}\eta, \dots, r_\text{max}\}$; ASHA (Li et al. 2018) promoting the moment $\eta$ observations land on a rung — eliminating SH's synchronization barrier; NAS / AutoML as natural HPO extensions. 5 key quotes (HPO-as-global-opt definition, random search as most-used HPO, random-beats-grid statement, linear async speed-up, ASHA promotion rule).
- New entity pages (6): [[AaronKlein]] (Amazon, first author), [[MatthiasSeeger]] (Amazon, co-author + SyneTune architect), [[CedricArchambeau]] (Amazon, co-author + Berlin AutoML group), [[JamesBergstra]] (random-vs-grid + TPE / HyperOpt), [[LishaLi]] (Hyperband + ASHA first author), [[SyneTune]] (Amazon's distributed HPO library — D2L's preferred backend).
- New concept pages (7): [[HyperparameterOptimization]] (umbrella concept, full algorithmic treatment), [[RandomSearch]] (Bergstra-Bengio justification + parallelism analysis), [[GridSearch]] (strict baseline), [[SuccessiveHalving]] (Jamieson-Karnin scheduler), [[Hyperband]] ($(n, r)$ bracket-hedging), [[MultiFidelityOptimization]] (the $f(\mathbf{x}, r)$ framing), [[BlackBoxOptimization]] (HPO's parent framing).
- Updated existing pages (substantive expansion + `sources:` bump): [[ASHA]] (full algorithmic detail of the promotion rule + synchronous-SH-failure-mode framing; sources bumped to `d2l-hyperparameter-optimization`); [[BayesianOptimization]] (surrogate / acquisition table + random-vs-BO comparison + cold-start / parallelism trade-offs; sources bumped); [[HyperparameterTuning]] (cross-link to the new [[HyperparameterOptimization]] page added as the canonical reference; sources bumped). Also updated [[Amazon]], [[RayTune]], [[Optuna]] entity pages to add `d2l-hyperparameter-optimization` to `sources:` and added one-line cross-references.
- Overview: §IX bumped 21 → 22 chapters; appended a substantial [[d2l-hyperparameter-optimization]] summary covering the black-box framing + searcher/scheduler API + random-vs-grid (Bergstra-Bengio) + random-vs-BO (Snoek) + async linear speed-up + multi-fidelity (SH / Hyperband / ASHA) + Li et al. 2018 ASHA promotion rule + NAS/AutoML extensions, positioning the chapter as the **non-learnable-parameter complement** to [[d2l-optimization]].

## [2026-05-16] ingest | D2L Generative Adversarial Networks

- Source: `raw/d2l-en/chapter_generative-adversarial-networks/` (gan.md + dcgan.md), slug `d2l-generative-adversarial-networks`; D2L's first dedicated generative-modeling chapter.
- 12 key claims covering: discriminative-vs-generative framing; the canonical $\min_D \max_G \{-E_x \log D(\mathbf{x}) - E_z \log(1-D(G(\mathbf{z})))\}$ minimax objective (Goodfellow et al. 2014); deliberately-minimal generator + "a bit more discriminating" 3-layer-MLP discriminator in the 2-D Gaussian toy; the non-saturating $\min_G\{-\log D(G(\mathbf{z}))\}$ reformulation that fixes vanishing gradients early in training; DCGAN's all-convolutional architecture (Radford, Metz & Chintala 2015); BN as structural to DCGAN stability; LeakyReLU $\alpha=0.2$ for $D$ + ReLU for $G$ (dying-ReLU fix); transposed convolution as the generator's upsampling primitive ($k=4, s=2, p=1 \Rightarrow 2\times$ upsampling); Pokémon dataset with `Normalize(0.5, 0.5)` matching $\tanh$ output range; Adam $\beta_1=0.5$ override (single most-cited GAN hyperparameter tweak); transposed-conv as "fractionally strided" via the $\mathbf{x}' = \mathbf{W}^\top \mathbf{y}'$ matrix view. 5 key quotes (foundational two-sample-test framing, the minimax objective, the non-saturating loss reformulation, LeakyReLU dying-ReLU motivation, Adam $\beta_1=0.5$ rationale).
- New entity pages (2): [[IanGoodfellow]] (GAN inventor; *Deep Learning* textbook co-author; FGSM author; career arc Google Brain → OpenAI → Apple → DeepMind), [[AlecRadford]] (DCGAN first author + GPT / GPT-2 / CLIP first author at OpenAI — one of the most influential generative-model authors of the 2015-2020 era).
- New concept pages (6): [[Generator]] (the "fake-sample synthesizer" half of a GAN; non-saturating loss reformulation; MLP-vs-DCGAN architecture taxonomy), [[Discriminator]] (the "two-sample classifier" half; BCE objective with `.detach()`; LeakyReLU rationale), [[MinMaxGame]] (the formal game-theoretic framing; Nash equilibrium $D \equiv 1/2$; why standard convergence theory does not apply), [[ModeCollapse]] (the dominant GAN failure mode; why it happens; mitigations from the broader literature), [[DCGAN]] (all-convolutional architecture; the four design rules; full architecture tables for D and G with shape math; training recipe), [[LeakyReLU]] (variant activation $\alpha \in [0,1]$; dying-ReLU fix; canonical $\alpha=0.2$ for GAN discriminators).
- Updated existing pages (substantive expansion + `sources:` bump): [[generativeadversarialnetwork]] (added full operational treatment — objective / non-saturating loss / DCGAN scale-up / training recipe / mode collapse; bumped to `[d2l-introduction, d2l-generative-adversarial-networks]`); [[TransposedConvolution]] (added DCGAN cross-link + bumped sources). Index entry for [[generativeadversarialnetwork]] also expanded.
- Overview: §IX bumped 22 → 23 chapters; appended a substantial [[d2l-generative-adversarial-networks]] summary covering the discriminative-vs-generative pivot, the GAN minimax objective + non-saturating loss reformulation, the DCGAN scale-up (architecture + LeakyReLU + Adam $\beta_1=0.5$), and the conceptual link to the [[DiffusionModel]] successor family.
- Contradictions: none direct. *Mild architectural drift*: D2L's DCGAN demo code applies BN uniformly across discriminator blocks; Radford et al. 2015 recommends omitting BN at the discriminator's input layer. Flagged on [[DCGAN]] and in the source-page Contradictions section. The note in [[generativeadversarialnetwork]] that diffusion models have replaced GANs in production text-to-image systems (DALL-E 2 / Imagen) remains accurate and is reinforced rather than contradicted.

derivative as local linearization + [[ChainRule|sum/product/chain]] rules + [[TaylorSeries|Taylor expansion]]; [[Gradient]] as steepest-direction via $\boldsymbol\epsilon\cdot\nabla L=\|\nabla L\|\cos\theta$ + critical points + [[Hessian]]; multivariate chain rule = [[Backpropagation]]; integral as area + [[FundamentalTheoremOfCalculus]] + change of variables; [[ProbabilityDensityFunction|PDFs]] / joint / marginal / conditional / [[Covariance]] / [[Correlation]]; [[MaximumLikelihoodEstimation|MLE]] = NLL minimization + Bayesian interpretation; asymptotic theory ([[FisherInformation]] + [[CramerRaoBound|Cramér-Rao]] + consistency / asymptotic normality / efficiency); [[NaiveBayes]] on binarized [[MNIST]] (~84% with conditional-independence + [[LaplaceSmoothing]] + log-space); [[Statistics|statistics]] ([[MeanSquaredError|MSE]] = [[BiasVarianceDecomposition|bias² + variance]], [[HypothesisTesting|hypothesis tests]] with type-I/II / power / *p*-values, [[ConfidenceInterval|CIs]]); [[InformationTheory|information theory]] ([[SelfInformation]] / [[Entropy|Shannon entropy]] / joint / conditional / [[MutualInformation|MI]] / [[KullbackLeiblerDivergence|KL]] / [[CrossEntropy|cross-entropy]] = $H(P)+D_{KL}(P\|Q)$ — the load-bearing identity behind every classification loss = MLE under categorical likelihood). 6 key quotes (framing the appendix as just-in-case math, $-\nabla L$ as steepest descent, matrices as skew-rotate-scale, dimension-agnostic angle formula, CLT as the "limit of any sum of small independent contributions", information theory as the cross-modal common language).

- New concept pages (14): [[Entropy]], [[KullbackLeiblerDivergence]], [[MutualInformation]], [[CrossEntropy]] (information-theoretic concept, distinct from the loss-function page [[CrossEntropyLoss]]), [[SelfInformation]], [[InformationTheory]] (parent / aggregator), [[NaiveBayes]], [[BernoulliDistribution]], [[CosineSimilarity]], [[Hyperplane]], [[EinsteinSummation]], [[CentralLimitTheorem]], [[ProbabilityDensityFunction]], [[HypothesisTesting]], [[ConfidenceInterval]], [[BiasVarianceDecomposition]], [[FisherInformation]] (17 total).

- Conceptual structure: (a) embedded is a spectrum (8-bit MCUs → Raspberry Pi-class SoCs); (b) two execution regimes — [[HostedEnvironment|hosted]] (POSIX-class, [[RustStandardLibrary|`libstd`]] available) vs [[BareMetalProgramming|bare-metal]] (no OS, no `libstd`); (c) [[NoStd|`#![no_std]`]] selects [[RustCoreLibrary|`libcore`]] in place of `libstd`; (d) losses: [[HeapAllocation|heap]], `Vec`, `HashMap`, stack-overflow protection, pre-`main` [[RustRuntime|runtime]]; gains: ability to write [[Bootloader|bootloaders]], firmware, kernels; (e) opt-in heap recipe — `alloc` crate + global allocator (e.g. `alloc-cortex-m`); `HashMap`/`HashSet` remain unavailable even then (no secure RNG in `core`).

Failure mode: bad `memory.x` lengths/origins → silent post-`continue` hang. Automation: the template's `openocd.gdb` script packs the whole connect+break+load+stepi sequence; `<gdb> -x openocd.gdb` runs it; the template's commented-out Cargo runner under `[target.'cfg(all(target_arch = "arm", target_os = "none"))']` turns `cargo run` into a one-shot real-hardware debug session — mirrors the QEMU-runner pattern from [[rust-embedded-book-start-qemu]]. - Source: `raw/book/src/start/registers.md` → [[rust-embedded-book-start-registers]]. ## [2026-05-16] ingest | The Embedded Rust Book — Exceptions

- Source: `raw/book/src/start/exceptions.md` → [[rust-embedded-book-start-exceptions]]. File 16/44 of *[[TheEmbeddedRustBook]]* — the *Getting Started* chapter's **Exceptions** sub-section, immediately after Panicking at file 15 ([[rust-embedded-book-start-panicking]]).
- Content: formalizes the [[ExceptionAttribute|`#[exception]`]] attribute as [[CortexMRTCrate|`cortex-m-rt`]]'s declarative surface for [[ARMCortexM|Cortex-M]] exception handlers ([[SysTick]], [[HardFault]], `NMI`, …, plus the catch-all [[DefaultHandler]]). Two non-obvious guarantees the chapter pins down: handlers are **software-uncallable** (calling `SysTick();` is a compile error) and `static mut` inside the body is therefore **safe** (the attribute rewrites it to `&mut`; soundness rests on the hardware-only invocation contract precluding reentrancy on a single core — multicore breaks this and needs explicit synchronization). Three worked examples: (1) [[SysTick]] handler counting ticks in a `COUNT` `static mut`, printing over [[ARMSemihosting|semihosting]] on [[QEMU]] via `qemu-system-arm -cpu cortex-m3 -machine lm3s6965evb`, self-terminating after 9 ticks via `debug::exit`; (2) `DefaultHandler(irqn: i16)` override pattern with the architectural-vs-device discriminant (`irqn < 0` ⇒ Cortex-M architectural exception, `irqn ≥ 0` ⇒ device-specific [[Interrupt|IRQ]]); (3) [[HardFault]] post-mortem with the mandatory `fn(&ExceptionFrame) -> !` signature, dumping the [[ExceptionFrame]] (r0…r12, lr, pc, xpsr) over semihosting and cross-referencing `pc = 0x0800094a` against `cargo objdump` to identify the faulting `ldr r0, [r0]` instruction reading `0x3fff_fffe`.
- Added **5 new pages** = 1 source + 4 concepts + 0 entities. Concepts: [[ExceptionAttribute]] (the `#[exception]` attribute itself; the two-guarantee soundness pair; the [[PanicHandlerAttribute]] sibling), [[DefaultHandler]] (the `#[no_mangle]` catch-all loop; the `irqn: i16` discriminant; centralization point for un-overridden exceptions), [[HardFault]] (the special exception with the forced divergent `&ExceptionFrame` signature; QEMU-vs-hardware caveat that `lm3s6965evb` does not check memory loads), [[ExceptionFrame]] (the register-snapshot struct mirroring the hardware-pushed stack frame; `pc` cross-referenced via `cargo objdump` is the diagnostic primitive).

- Source: `raw/book/src/peripherals/borrowck.md` → [[rust-embedded-book-peripherals-borrowck]]. - Content: pivot chapter. States three rules for safe [[Peripheral|peripheral]] access — (1) always [[VolatileMemoryAccess|volatile]]; (2) any number of read-only holders may share; (3) read-write requires exclusive ownership — and observes that rules (2) and (3) are **exactly** Rust's [[BorrowChecker|borrow checker]] discipline (`&T` shared, `&mut T` exclusive). The bridge: if each [[Peripheral|peripheral]] is represented by **exactly one** Rust value, the [[BorrowChecker|borrow checker]] gives safe sharing for free. Closes on the "exactly one instance" precondition — hardware is naturally unique but the code must mirror that uniqueness — deferred to the next sub-section's singleton pattern (file 21/44).

- Source: [[rust-embedded-book-portability-index]] (file 27/44 of *[[TheEmbeddedRustBook]]*; **opens** and is the sole content file of the single-file *Portability* chapter, **resolving** the long-standing forward references from [[rust-embedded-book-start-registers]] and [[EmbeddedHalCrate]] that the corpus has carried since the file-13 ingest).
- Chapter names [[Portability]] as the *"very important topic"* of embedded development (vendor + family + peripheral fragmentation), pins the standard solution as a [[HardwareAbstractionLayer|HAL]] (quoting the Wikipedia definition), and explains why the OS-syscall flavor of HALs fails for embedded (*"firmware images compiled as a whole … no operating systems"*). Introduces [[EmbeddedHalCrate|`embedded-hal`]] as *"a set of traits which define implementation contracts between HAL implementations, drivers and applications."* The chapter's central architectural payoff is the **M·N → M+N complexity collapse** (M HAL implementations × N drivers without `embedded-hal` → M+N with it). Six named trait families: [[GPIO]] input/output, serial, [[I2C]], [[SPI]], timers/countdowns, ADC. Three-role user taxonomy: [[HALCrate|HAL implementation]] (chip-specific, four flavors — low-level registers, OS-syscall like Linux sysfs, mock-for-testing, adapter-as-HAL like an I2C-multiplexer), [[DriverCrate|driver]] (chip-agnostic, trait-bounded generic, three component categories — sensors / displays / actuators), and application (the layer where port-time effort concentrates — *"the part which requires the most adaptation efforts"*).

No code example. ## [2026-05-16] ingest | The Embedded Rust Book — HAL GPIO
- Source: `raw/book/src/design-patterns/hal/gpio.md` → `wiki/sources/rust-embedded-book-design-patterns-hal-gpio.md` (file 36/44 of *[[TheEmbeddedRustBook]]* — **fourth and final leaf-section** of the *HAL Design Patterns* sub-chapter, the **GPIO Interfaces** group of the [[rust-embedded-book-design-patterns-hal-checklist|HAL Checklist]]; **by far the most substantive** of the four leaves — ~200 lines, four code blocks, three named patterns covering 3 of 8 HAL Checklist items).
- Content: three named patterns. **`C-ZST-PIN`** — *"GPIO Interfaces … should provide dedicated zero-sized types for each pin on every interface or port, resulting in a zero-cost GPIO abstraction when all pin assignments are statically known"*; each port implements a `split()` method returning a struct whose fields are one [[ZeroSizedType|ZST]] per physical pin — operationalizes [[ZeroSizedType]] / [[ZeroCostAbstraction]] from [[rust-embedded-book-static-guarantees-zero-cost-abstractions]] for [[GPIO]] pins. **`C-ERASED-PIN`** — two-stage type erasure: `PA0::erase_pin(self) -> PA { pin: u8 }` collapses 16 distinct ZSTs into a 1-byte handle (pin identity → runtime); `PA::erase_port(self) -> Pin { port: Port, pin: u8 }` collapses 4 ports into a ≤2-byte handle (port identity → runtime); trades compile-time-pin-distinctness for heterogeneous-collection-ability — `[Pin; N]` / `Vec<Pin>` / `&dyn` trait objects. **`C-PIN-STATE`** — two-axis [[TypeStateProgramming|typestate]] (`Input<Floating|PullUp|PullDown>` and `Output<PushPull|OpenDrain>` via `PhantomData<S>`), with **`into_input` / `into_output`** consuming-`self` transitions **plus** **`with_input_state` / `with_output_state`** scoped-reconfiguration variants (`&mut self` + `FnOnce(&mut PA1<N>) -> R`) for *"temporarily reconfigur[ing] a pin in a different state without moving it"* — the addition over a vanilla builder-style typestate; all bounded by **sealed traits** so users cannot add their own pin states. The three patterns **stack**: a fully-static `PA1<Output<PushPull>>` is a zero-sized handle that survives both erasure stages without losing the typestate parameter — *"both erased and non-erased pin types should provide the same API."*
- New pages: **2** — [[ErasedPin]] (the type-erased pin abstraction) and [[PinTypeState]] (the two-axis pin typestate). Reuses [[TheEmbeddedRustBook]], [[GPIO]], [[HALCrate]], [[TypeStateProgramming]], [[ZeroSizedType]], [[ZeroCostAbstraction]], [[Singleton]], [[BuilderPattern]], plus parent chapter pages.
- Updated pages: [[GPIO]] — added [[rust-embedded-book-design-patterns-hal-gpio]] to `sources` frontmatter and a new Connections bullet pointing to [[PinTypeState]] / [[ErasedPin]].

## [2026-05-16] ingest | The Embedded Rust Book — Interoperability
- Source: `raw/book/src/interoperability/index.md` → `wiki/sources/rust-embedded-book-interoperability-index.md` (file 38/44 of *[[TheEmbeddedRustBook]]* — **opens the *Interoperability* chapter**, Part 6's second chapter, immediately after the [[rust-embedded-book-c-tips-index|Tips for embedded C developers]] chapter forward-referenced *"A little C with your Rust / A little Rust with your C"*).
- Content: short three-section framing page (~30 lines). (1) The **`std::ffi` family** — C primitive type aliases (`c_uint` ≡ `unsigned int`, `c_void` ≡ `void`, `c_char`, `c_long`, …) and string conversions (`CString` for `String` → `char *`; `CStr` for `&str` → `const char *`); mirrored in `core::ffi` ([[NoStd|`no_std`]], no allocator) and `alloc::ffi` (alloc required) since Rust 1.30; [`cty`] and [`cstr_core`] crates as community equivalents for older toolchains. Type table: `String` ↔ `CString` ↔ `char *`; `&str` ↔ `CStr` ↔ `const char *`; `()` ↔ `c_void` ↔ `void`; `u32`/`u64` ↔ `c_uint` ↔ `unsigned int`. Worked example: `let c_num: c_uint = num; let r_num: u32 = c_num;` compiles where `unsigned int` is 32-bit because `c_uint` is a **type alias**, not a wrapper. (2) **Build-system interop** — combining [[Cargo]] with Make / CMake is open work (tracked in `rust-embedded/book#61`); no recipe. (3) **RTOS interop** — *"still a work in progress; especially calling RTOS functions from Rust can be tricky"*; only the **Zephyr Project** publicly supports Rust↔RTOS integration (tracked in `#62`).
